SKILL ---\n---\nname: dra-compute-domain-imex-workflows\ndescription: Explain, design, review, or troubleshoot NVIDIA DRA ComputeDomain workflows across MNNVL hardware, IMEX daemon lifecycle, driver-managed or host-managed mode, ComputeDomain and ComputeDomainClique resources, per-domain DaemonSets, channel claims, readiness, kubelet prepare, and workload device or mount injection. Use when asked about GB200 or GB300 fabrics, clique state, IMEX channels, or multi-node workload failures.\n---\n\n# DRA ComputeDomain and IMEX Workflows\n\n## Purpose\n\nTrace a ComputeDomain from custom-resource creation through IMEX coordination and channel preparation to the exact workload-visible device and mount.\n\n## Triggers\n\n- Explain or create a ComputeDomain and channel-consuming workload.\n- Diagnose IMEX daemon, clique, readiness, channel claim, controller, DNS, or MNNVL failures.\n- Review driver-managed versus host-managed IMEX behavior.\n- Analyze ComputeDomain CRDs, controller reconciliation, daemon lifecycle, or channel injection.\n\n## Audience\n\n- Contributors\n- Cluster operators\n- Support engineers\n\n## Inputs\n\n- DRA driver/chart, Kubernetes, GPU Operator, and NVIDIA driver versions\n- MNNVL system, clique topology/labels, driver root, GFD, and IMEX package/service state\n- Helm `resources.computeDomains` values and feature gates\n- ComputeDomain, ComputeDomainClique, DaemonSet, ResourceClaimTemplate, ResourceClaim, Pod, event, and log evidence\n- Selected IMEX mode, isolation, socket path, and channel allocation mode\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Verify current behavior from `site/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md`, `site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md`, prerequisites, API docs, chart values, and code.\n2. Confirm the environment floor and MNNVL facts. Check expected `nvidia.com/gpu.clique` labels, participating nodes, driver/GFD state, and IMEX service ownership.\n3. Determine the IMEX lifecycle mode from Helm values and feature gates:\n   - `driverManaged`: the driver creates per-ComputeDomain daemon workload and owns its lifecycle.\n   - `hostManaged`: the cluster operator owns host `nvidia-imex`; verify the gate, mode, isolation, control socket, `nvidia-imex-ctl`, and readiness contract in the current checkout.\n4. Trace the driver-managed control path:\n   - A user creates `resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1` `ComputeDomain`.\n   - `cmd/compute-domain-controller/` reconciles it, creates the per-domain DaemonSet and channel/daemon claim templates, and maintains related state.\n   - `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/` supervises `nvidia-imex` on participating nodes.\n   - Daemons publish membership, address, clique, index, and readiness through `ComputeDomainClique` resources when that gate is active.\n5. Trace the workload path:\n   - The workload references the controller-created channel `ResourceClaimTemplate`.\n   - Kubernetes allocates a `compute-domain-default-channel.nvidia.com` device.\n   - Kubelet calls `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/` prepare.\n   - The plugin verifies local IMEX readiness and claim/domain relationship.\n   - CDI/runtime setup injects `/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/chan*` and `/imexd` as required.\n6. Treat `ComputeDomain.status` as informational unless the current code/docs explicitly define a gating contract. Use claim preparation and local daemon readiness as the workload boundary.\n7. Distinguish `Single` and `All` channel allocation modes and verify expected channel count.\n8. Diagnose in layers: chart/component enablement → clique labels/topology → controller reconciliation → DaemonSet or host service → ComputeDomainClique/readiness → ResourceSlice/DeviceClass → channel claim allocation → plugin prepare → injected device/mount.\n9. Check feature-gate dependencies in code, especially clique/DNS and host-managed behavior. Report code/docs drift rather than guessing.\n10. For upgrades or mode changes, protect active ComputeDomains and claims. Treat driver-managed/host-managed transitions and checkpoint changes as explicit migration work.\n11. Return a source-backed sequence or triage report with versions, mode, topology, evidence, failure boundary, and safe next checks.\n\n## Expected Outputs\n\n- ComputeDomain/IMEX sequence explanation\n- Workload or channel-claim design\n- Bounded multi-node triage report\n- Mode and upgrade compatibility checklist\n\n## References\n\n- ComputeDomain concepts: `site/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md`\n- Architecture: `site/content/docs/concepts/architecture.md`\n- Workload guide: `site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md`\n- Prerequisites: `site/content/docs/prerequisites.md`\n- API reference: `site/content/docs/reference/api.md`\n- Helm authority: `deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/`\n- Implementation: `cmd/compute-domain-controller/`, `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/`, `pkg/imex/`\n- API types: `api/nvidia.com/resource/v1beta1/`\n- Public NVIDIA ComputeDomain background: https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/cloud-native/gpu-operator/latest/dra-cds.html\n- Public GPU Operator DRA install guide: https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/cloud-native/gpu-operator/latest/dra-intro-install.html\n- Internal supplemental: none provided by this skill\n\n## Validation\n\n- Confirm mode, feature gates, IMEX service owner, socket/readiness path, isolation, allocation mode, and clique topology.\n- Verify the controller-created objects and owner references match the selected mode.\n- Verify every expected node or clique has readiness evidence.\n- Verify the channel claim is allocated and the plugin prepare path injects the expected channel device and `/imexd`.\n- For the ADR scenario, explain ComputeDomain creation → controller reconciliation → IMEX daemon supervision → clique state → channel claim → workload injection.\n- Separate driver defects from GFD labels, NVIDIA driver/IMEX package, host service, Kubernetes DRA, runtime, DNS, and hardware fabric issues.\n\n## Safety Constraints\n\n- Use read-only inspection first and obtain explicit approval before applying/deleting ComputeDomains, changing host IMEX service state, switching modes, restarting components, or running MNNVL tests.\n- Never expose kubeconfigs, credentials, customer topology, private node addresses, or sensitive logs.\n- Avoid mode changes while active ComputeDomain workloads exist unless an approved drain and recovery plan is present.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Do not claim ComputeDomains work on non-MNNVL hardware.\n- Do not treat status alone as proof that a workload channel is prepared.\n- Do not bypass operator ownership of host-managed IMEX.\n\n## Owners\n\n- DRA ComputeDomain engineering\n- DRA support\n\n## Refresh Policy\n\n- Review every DRA driver and GPU Operator release.\n- Review after ComputeDomain CRD, clique, DNS, IMEX mode/isolation, driver prerequisite, MNNVL platform, or checkpoint changes.\n- Verify current online prerequisites before operator-facing guidance.\n\n## Registration Notes\n\nKeep this `SKILL.md` as the shared source of truth. Use `agents/openai.yaml` only as registration metadata.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

It is used to enable GPU memory sharing across nodes in hardware that supports Multi-Node NVLink (MNNVL), such as GB200 NVL72 or H100 NVLink configurations.\n\n---\n\n## IMEX lifecycle modes\n\nThe `resources.computeDomains.imex.mode` Helm value determines who manages the `nvidia-imex` daemon lifecycle.\n\n| Mode | Lifecycle |\n|---|---|\n| `driverManaged` | By default, the DRA Driver creates one `nvidia-imex` DaemonSet for each `ComputeDomain` and tracks daemon readiness through `ComputeDomainClique` resources. |\n| `hostManaged` | You run `nvidia-imex` as a host service with a ready command socket, and the DRA Driver does not create daemon DaemonSets or daemon claims; this mode requires the `HostManagedIMEXDaemon` feature gate. |\n\n## How driver-managed mode works\n\nCreating a `ComputeDomain` in the default `driverManaged` mode triggers the following sequence:\n\n1. The `compute-domain-controller` watches for new `ComputeDomain` resources and creates a per-domain DaemonSet.\n2. Each daemon pod in that DaemonSet runs `nvidia-imex`, which manages the NVLink fabric connection on its node.\n3. Each daemon publishes its IP address, clique membership, and readiness via a `ComputeDomainClique` CR in the driver namespace.\n4. The `compute-domain-controller` also creates a `ResourceClaimTemplate` per channel, making IMEX channels available for workload pods to claim.\n5. When a workload pod claims a channel, the `compute-domain-kubelet-plugin` injects one or more selected IMEX channel devices (`/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channelN`) into the container.\n\nFor the full sequence diagram, see [Architecture › Driver-managed ComputeDomain flow](architecture.md#driver-managed-computedomain-flow).\n\n## How host-managed mode works\n\nCreating a `ComputeDomain` in `hostManaged` mode uses an existing host `nvidia-imex` service:\n\n1. You run `nvidia-imex` on each participating GPU node and expose its command socket.\n2. The `compute-domain-controller` creates the workload `ResourceClaimTemplate`.\n3. When a workload pod claims a channel, the `compute-domain-kubelet-plugin` queries the host daemon through the daemon's command socket and requires a `READY` response. If the daemon is unavailable or not ready, claim preparation fails and is retried.\n4. After the readiness check succeeds, the plugin injects channel 0 (`/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channel0`) into the workload container.\n5. Deleting the `ComputeDomain` removes the workload claim template, but it does not stop or reconfigure the host service.\n\nHost-managed mode currently supports domain isolation only. All ComputeDomains\nthat use the same host IMEX domain share channel 0. Because this mode has no\nper-ComputeDomain daemon pods, the controller disables its\n`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` and `ComputeDomainCliques` behaviors and does not create\n`ComputeDomainClique` objects. A `Ready` ComputeDomain therefore does not report\nthe health of the host service.\n\nFor service and socket configuration, see [Host-managed IMEX](../prerequisites.md#host-managed-imex).\n\n---\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nSee [Prerequisites](../prerequisites.md) for hardware and software requirements, including the ComputeDomain-specific requirements for Multi-Node NVLink hardware, `nvidia.com/gpu.clique` label ownership, and `nvidia-imex` service configuration.\n\n---\n\n## Get started\n\nTo create a `ComputeDomain` and run a workload that claims an IMEX channel, see the [ComputeDomain workloads guide](../guides/compute-domain-workloads.md).\n\n--- FILE site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md ---\n---\ntitle: ComputeDomain workloads\nlinkTitle: ComputeDomain workloads\nweight: 30\ndescription: Create a ComputeDomain, claim a channel, and run a Multi-Node NVLink workload.\n---\n\nFor background on what a `ComputeDomain` is and how it fits together, see\n[ComputeDomains](../concepts/compute-domains.md).\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nRefer to [Prerequisites](../prerequisites.md) for hardware and software requirements, including the ComputeDomain-specific requirements for Multi-Node NVLink hardware, `nvidia.com/gpu.clique` label ownership, and `nvidia-imex` service configuration.\n\n## Create a ComputeDomain\n\nThe minimal `ComputeDomain` spec requires only the name of the `ResourceClaimTemplate` the controller will create for channel allocation:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: ComputeDomain\nmetadata:\n  name: my-compute-domain\nspec:\n  numNodes: 0\n  channel:\n    resourceClaimTemplate:\n      name: imex-channel-0\n```\n\n`numNodes` is deprecated. Set it to `0` (the recommended value when `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` is enabled — its default state).\n\nAfter applying this resource, the controller creates:\n\n- A per-domain `DaemonSet` of `compute-domain-daemon` pods, one per GPU node.\n- A `ResourceClaimTemplate` named `imex-channel-0`, which workload pods use to request a channel.\n\nThese objects describe the default `driverManaged` mode.\nIn `hostManaged` mode, the controller creates the workload `ResourceClaimTemplate` but does not create the per-domain daemon DaemonSet.\nThe host service must be ready through its configured command socket before a workload claim can prepare.\nHost-managed mode currently provides domain isolation and channel 0 only, even if the `ComputeDomain` requests `allocationMode: All`.\nSee [Host-managed IMEX](../prerequisites.md#host-managed-imex) before you select this mode.\n\n## Use the channel in a workload\n\nReference the `ResourceClaimTemplate` name you set in `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate.name` when writing your workload:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: v1\nkind: Pod\nmetadata:\n  name: my-workload\nspec:\n  affinity:\n    nodeAffinity:\n      requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:\n        nodeSelectorTerms:\n        - matchExpressions:\n          - key: nvidia.com/gpu.clique\n            operator: Exists\n  containers:\n  - name: app\n    image: ubuntu:22.04\n    command: [\"bash\", \"-c\"]\n    args:\n    - |\n      set -eu\n      test -c /dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channel0\n      echo \"IMEX channel0 is available\"\n      ls -la /dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels\n      sleep 9999\n    resources:\n      claims:\n      - name: imex-channel-0\n  resourceClaims:\n  - name: imex-channel-0\n    resourceClaimTemplateName: imex-channel-0\n```\n\nThe required node affinity prevents the workload from being used as a false-positive\ntest on a node that is not part of an NVLink clique.\nThe pod will not start until the local IMEX daemon is ready, and its startup command\nfails unless `channel0` is a character device.\nAfter the pod starts, verify the device explicitly; `Running` by itself is not a\nsuccessful IMEX validation:\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec my-workload -- test -c /dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channel0\nkubectl logs my-workload\n```\n\nThe first command must exit successfully, and the logs must include\n`IMEX channel0 is available`.\n\n### Channel allocation modes\n\nIn `driverManaged` mode, the `spec.channel.allocationMode` field controls how many\nIMEX channels are injected:\n\n| Mode | Value | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| Single | `Single` (default) | Injects a single IMEX channel into the workload container |\n| All | `All` | Injects all available IMEX channels (up to the hardware maximum) |\n\nUse `All` only in `driverManaged` mode for workloads that need access to every\nchannel in the IMEX domain.\nIn `hostManaged` mode, domain isolation shares channel 0 among workloads.\nThe controller forces the generated workload claim to `Single`, so a requested\n`All` is equivalent to `Single` when using host-managed mode and does not expose additional channels.\n\n## Check status\n\n```bash\nkubectl get computedomain my-compute-domain -o yaml\n```\n\nInterpret `status.status` according to the configured lifecycle mode:\n\n- In `driverManaged` mode, `Ready` reflects the readiness of the per-ComputeDomain\n  daemons managed by the driver. When `ComputeDomainCliques` is enabled, inspect\n  the `ComputeDomainClique` objects for daemon membership:\n\n  ```bash\n  kubectl get computedomainclique -n dra-driver-nvidia-gpu\n  ```\n\n- In `hostManaged` mode, `Ready` means only that the controller admitted the\n  `ComputeDomain` and created its workload `ResourceClaimTemplate`. It does not\n  report the health of the administrator-managed `nvidia-imex` service.\n  Host daemon health is checked later, on each node, when the kubelet plugin\n  prepares a workload channel claim. Host-managed mode does not create\n  `ComputeDomainClique` objects.\n\n## Validate host-managed IMEX\n\nUse this validation after installing the driver with both\n`featureGates.HostManagedIMEXDaemon=true` and\n`resources.computeDomains.imex.mode=hostManaged`.\nComplete the [host-managed prerequisites](../prerequisites.md#host-managed-imex)\nfirst, including configuring the same command socket path in the host service and\nthe Helm value.\n\n1. On every node that can run the workload, verify the administrator-managed\n   service and command socket:\n\n   ```bash\n   sudo systemctl is-active nvidia-imex.service\n   sudo /usr/bin/nvidia-imex-ctl -q --u=/etc/nvidia-imex/imex_ctrl.sock\n   ```\n\n   The first command must report `active` and the second must report `READY`.\n   Substitute the configured `resources.computeDomains.imex.hostSocketPath` when\n   it is not the default.\n\n2. Create the `ComputeDomain` shown above, then verify that the controller created\n   only its workload claim template:\n\n   ```bash\n   CD_UID=\"$(kubectl get computedomain my-compute-domain -o jsonpath='{.metadata.uid}')\"\n\n   kubectl get resourceclaimtemplate imex-channel-0\n   kubectl get daemonset --all-namespaces \\\n       -l \"resource.nvidia.com/computeDomain=${CD_UID}\"\n   kubectl get resourceclaimtemplate --all-namespaces \\\n       -l \"resource.nvidia.com/computeDomain=${CD_UID},resource.nvidia.com/computeDomainTarget=Daemon\"\n   ```\n\n   The first command must return `imex-channel-0`. The two label queries must\n   return no resources: host-managed mode creates neither a per-domain daemon\n   DaemonSet nor a daemon `ResourceClaimTemplate`.\n\n3. Save the clique-affined workload above as `my-workload.yaml`, apply it, and\n   verify `channel0` with both `test -c` and the workload log commands shown\n   after the manifest.\n   A `Running` pod without that device check is not sufficient.\n\n4. In a maintenance window on a test node, exercise readiness failure and\n   recovery. Record the node used in step 3, then delete that pod:\n\n   ```bash\n   TEST_NODE=\"$(kubectl get pod my-workload -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')\"\n   echo \"${TEST_NODE}\"\n   kubectl delete -f my-workload.yaml\n   ```\n\n   In `my-workload.yaml`, add this expression alongside the existing\n   `nvidia.com/gpu.clique` expression, replacing `<test-node-name>` with the\n   value printed above:\n\n   ```yaml\n   - key: kubernetes.io/hostname\n     operator: In\n     values:\n     - <test-node-name>\n   ```\n\n   On that node, stop `nvidia-imex.service`:\n\n   ```bash\n   sudo systemctl stop nvidia-imex.service\n   ```\n\n   Reapply the same pod manifest from your administration host:\n\n   ```bash\n   kubectl apply -f my-workload.yaml\n   kubectl describe pod my-workload\n   kubectl get events --field-selector reason=FailedPrepareDynamicResources \\\n       --sort-by=.lastTimestamp\n   ```\n\n   The pod must not start while the command socket is unavailable: claim\n   preparation fails and the kubelet retries. On the same node, restart the\n   service and wait for the command socket to report `READY`. The same pending\n   pod and claim then proceed without being recreated:\n\n   ```bash\n   sudo systemctl start nvidia-imex.service\n   sudo /usr/bin/nvidia-imex-ctl -q --u=/etc/nvidia-imex/imex_ctrl.sock\n   ```\n\n   From your administration host, verify recovery and channel injection:\n\n   ```bash\n   kubectl wait --for=condition=Ready pod/my-workload --timeout=5m\n   kubectl exec my-workload -- test -c /dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channel0\n   ```\n\n5. Delete the workload and `ComputeDomain`. The controller removes the workload\n   claim template, but it does not stop or reconfigure the host service:\n\n   ```bash\n   kubectl delete pod my-workload\n   kubectl delete computedomain my-compute-domain\n   ```\n\n   Keep `nvidia-imex.service` running and manage its lifecycle outside the DRA\n   Driver.\n\n## Feature gates\n\nThe default driver-managed mode uses two Beta feature gates that are enabled by default.\n\n| Feature gate | Stage | Default | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` | Beta | `true` | Makes daemons communicate using DNS names instead of raw IP addresses and is required by `ComputeDomainCliques`. |\n| `ComputeDomainCliques` | Beta | `true` | Uses `ComputeDomainClique` CRD objects to track daemon membership per clique instead of storing that information in `ComputeDomain.status.nodes` and requires `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`. |\n| `HostManagedIMEXDaemon` | Alpha | `false` | Allows you to select `resources.computeDomains.imex.mode=hostManaged` without changing the lifecycle mode by itself. |\n\nTo disable a Beta gate (for example, to test a downgrade path):\n\n```yaml\nfeatureGates:\n  ComputeDomainCliques: false\n  IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames: false\n```\n\nSee [Feature gates](../reference/feature-gates/) for all available gates.\n\n--- FILE site/content/docs/reference/api.md ---\n---\ntitle: API reference\nlinkTitle: API reference\nweight: 10\ndescription: Types and fields for the resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1 API group.\n---\n\nAll types belong to API group `resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1`.\n\n## GPU opaque configuration types\n\nThese types are set as opaque configuration in `ResourceClaim` and `ResourceClaimTemplate` specs to configure GPU resources. They are optional; omit them to use driver defaults. Some fields require feature gates to be enabled.\n\n### GpuConfig\n\nConfigures a full GPU device. \nTarget DeviceClass: `gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n  timeSlicingConfig:\n    interval: Default       # Default | Short | Medium | Long\n```\n\nWith MPS (Multi-Process Service):\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50             # optional, integer\n    defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit: \"4Gi\"         # optional, quantity\n    multiUser: true                               # optional, default false\n    defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit:            # optional, map\n      \"0\": \"2Gi\"\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Sharing strategy and its configuration. Omit to use the device exclusively. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS` (Multi-Process Service). |\n| `sharing.timeSlicingConfig.interval` | string | Time-slice duration: `Default`, `Short`, `Medium`, or `Long`. Requires the [`TimeSlicingSettings`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultActiveThreadPercentage` | integer | Thread percentage limit applied to all processes sharing the GPU. Requires the [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit` | quantity | Pinned memory limit applied to all devices. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit` | map | Per-device override of `defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit`. Keys are device index (integer) or UUID string. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.multiUser` | bool | Starts the MPS control daemon in multi-user mode so processes with different user IDs can share the device, defaults to `false` when omitted, and requires a Volta or newer GPU. |\n\n\n### MigDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a MIG device slice. Target DeviceClass: `mig.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n```\n\nWith MPS:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50\n    multiUser: true\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Supports `TimeSlicing` and `MPS` strategies. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS`. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig` | object | Supports the same MPS fields, defaults, and Volta-or-newer `multiUser` requirement as `GpuConfig.sharing.mpsConfig` and requires [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md). |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\n`timeSlicingConfig` is not available for MIG devices. Time-slicing on MIG slices does not support interval configuration.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### VfioDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a VFIO passthrough device. Target DeviceClass: `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: VfioDeviceConfig\niommu:\n  backendPolicy: LegacyOnly   # LegacyOnly | PreferIommuFD\n  enableAPIDevice: false       # optional\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `iommu` | object | Optional. IOMMU backend configuration. Omit to use defaults (`LegacyOnly`, API device disabled). |\n| `iommu.backendPolicy` | string | `LegacyOnly` (default) or `PreferIommuFD`. Selects the IOMMU backend used for device passthrough. |\n| `iommu.enableAPIDevice` | bool | Optional. Expose `/dev/iommu` or `/dev/vfio/vfio` to the workload. Defaults to `false`. Requires the [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`PassthroughSupport`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false). `iommu.enableAPIDevice` additionally requires [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n## ComputeDomain CRDs\n\nComputeDomains use two Custom Resource Definitions. Unlike the GPU opaque types above, these are concrete Kubernetes resources that exist independently of `ResourceClaim` specs.\n\n### ComputeDomain\n\nA user-created resource that provisions an ephemeral multi-node NVLink fabric. Creating one triggers the controller to deploy a per-domain IMEX daemon fleet and generate `ResourceClaimTemplate` objects that workload pods reference to claim IMEX channels. The spec is immutable after creation.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: ComputeDomain\nmetadata:\n  name: my-compute-domain\nspec:\n  numNodes: 0\n  channel:\n    resourceClaimTemplate:\n      name: my-channel\n    allocationMode: Single        # Single (default) | All\n```\n\n#### Spec fields\n\n| Field | Type | Default | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `spec.numNodes` | integer | — | Deprecated. Set to `0` when using the default [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. Formerly used to gate workload startup until a specific number of IMEX daemons joined. |\n| `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate.name` | string | — | Name of the `ResourceClaimTemplate` the controller creates for workload pods to claim a channel from. |\n| `spec.channel.allocationMode` | string | `Single` | `Single` allocates one IMEX channel per claim. `All` allocates the maximum number of channels available in the IMEX domain. |\n\n#### Status fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `status.status` | string | `Ready` when all expected IMEX daemons have joined; `NotReady` otherwise. |\n| `status.nodes[].name` | string | Node name. |\n| `status.nodes[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `status.nodes[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier for the node. |\n| `status.nodes[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Used to map IPs to DNS names. |\n| `status.nodes[].status` | string | Per-node daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nDo not gate workload startup on `status.status`. The status field is informational. IMEX daemons start as soon as their local node joins without waiting for all peers.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### ComputeDomainClique\n\nA `ComputeDomainClique` is a driver-managed resource created automatically by the IMEX daemon on each node. You do not create or modify these directly.\n\nEach `ComputeDomainClique` is namespaced to the driver namespace and named as `<computeDomainUID>.<cliqueID>`. It tracks which nodes have joined a specific NVLink clique within a `ComputeDomain` and their daemon readiness.\n\nThe ComputeDomain plugin reads these objects to verify that the local IMEX daemon is ready before allowing a workload container to start.\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`ComputeDomainCliques`](feature-gates.md) (Beta, default: true), which depends on [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `daemons[].nodeName` | string | Node name. |\n| `daemons[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `daemons[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier. |\n| `daemons[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Maps to a DNS name. |\n| `daemons[].status` | string | Daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. Defaults to `NotReady`. |\n\n\n## ComputeDomain opaque configuration types\n\nThe following opaque types are set in the `ResourceClaim` specs generated by the ComputeDomain controller. They are managed automatically, you do not set these directly. They are documented here for reference and debugging.\n\n### ComputeDomainChannelConfig\n\nOpaque configuration for IMEX channel `ResourceClaim` objects. Set by the controller in the `ResourceClaimTemplate` it generates from `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate`.\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `domainID` | string | UID of the parent `ComputeDomain` resource. |\n| `allocationMode` | string | Inherited from `spec.channel.allocationMode`. `Single` or `All`. May be absent when `Single` (the default). |\n\n### ComputeDomainDaemonConfig\n\nOpaque configuration for IMEX daemon `ResourceClaim` objects. Set by the controller in the daemon DaemonSet.\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `domainID` | string | UID of the parent `ComputeDomain` resource. |\n\n--- FILE site/content/docs/reference/feature-gates.md ---\n---\ntitle: Feature gates\nlinkTitle: Feature gates\nweight: 20\ndescription: Available feature gates, their stages, defaults, and constraints.\n---\n\nFeature gates control experimental and beta functionality in the DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs. They follow [Kubernetes feature gate conventions](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/command-line-tools-reference/feature-gates/).\n\n## Set feature gates\n\nSet feature gates in your Helm values file:\n\n```yaml\nfeatureGates:\n  TimeSlicingSettings: true\n  MPSSupport: true\n```\n\nOr pass them at install time:\n\n```bash\nhelm install dra-driver-nvidia-gpu oci://registry.k8s.io/dra-driver-nvidia/charts/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu \\\n    --version {{< param \"driver_version\" >}} \\\n    --namespace dra-driver-nvidia-gpu \\\n    --set \"featureGates.TimeSlicingSettings=true\"\n```\n\n## Available feature gates\n\nThe Kubernetes column identifies additional Kubernetes version or Kubernetes feature gate requirements beyond the baseline versions in [Prerequisites](../prerequisites.md).\n\n| Feature gate | Stage | Default | Kubernetes | Description |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `TimeSlicingSettings` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables customization of CUDA time-slicing settings in `GpuConfig`. |\n| `MPSSupport` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables Multi-Process Service (MPS) sharing strategy in `GpuConfig` and `MigDeviceConfig`. |\n| `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | IMEX daemons use DNS names instead of raw IP addresses for peer communication. Required by `ComputeDomainCliques`. |\n| `PassthroughSupport` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables VFIO passthrough device allocation using `VfioDeviceConfig`. |\n| `DynamicMIG` | Alpha | `false` | v1.34 through v1.35 requires `DRAPartitionableDevices=true` on the kube-apiserver and kube-scheduler.  v1.36 and later enables `DRAPartitionableDevices` by default. See [Kubernetes feature gates](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/command-line-tools-reference/feature-gates/#DRAPartitionableDevices). | Enables dynamic MIG device allocation and reconfiguration. See [MIG](../guides/gpu-allocation/mig.md). |\n| `NVMLDeviceHealthCheck` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables GPU health checking using NVML. |\n| `ComputeDomainCliques` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | Uses `ComputeDomainClique` CRD objects to track IMEX daemon membership. Requires `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`. |\n| `CrashOnNVLinkFabricErrors` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | Causes the kubelet plugin to crash rather than fall back to non-fabric mode when NVLink fabric errors are detected. |\n| `DeviceMetadata` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables IOMMU API device exposure, such as `/dev/iommu` or `/dev/vfio/vfio`, for VFIO workloads with `VfioDeviceConfig`. Requires `PassthroughSupport`. |\n| `FabricManagerPartitioning` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables Fabric Manager partition discovery and lifecycle management for full GPUs and VFIO devices on supported HGX and single-node NVL systems. VFIO devices require `PassthroughSupport`. Requires Fabric Manager with `FABRIC_MODE=1`. Each full-GPU or VFIO claim on a participating node must exactly match one published partition. See [Fabric Manager partitioning](../guides/gpu-allocation/fabric-manager-partitioning.md). |\n| `HostManagedIMEXDaemon` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Allows `resources.computeDomains.imex.mode=hostManaged`, where you manage the host `nvidia-imex` service instead of the DRA Driver creating per-ComputeDomain daemon DaemonSets. This gate only unlocks the mode. Driver-managed DNS daemon naming and `ComputeDomainClique` tracking are not used in this mode. See [Validate host-managed IMEX](../guides/compute-domain-workloads.md#validate-host-managed-imex). |\n| `DRAListTypeAttributes` | Alpha | `false` | v1.36 or later with `DRAListTypeAttributes=true` on the kube-apiserver and kube-scheduler. | Publishes list-valued DRA device attributes, including `resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode` as a one-element list. See [NUMA locality](resourceslice-attributes.md#numa-locality). |\n| `ConsumableShares` | Alpha | `false` | v1.34 through v1.35 requires `DRAConsumableCapacity=true` on the kube-apiserver, kube-controller-manager, kube-scheduler, and kubelet; v1.36 and later enables `DRAConsumableCapacity` by default; see [Kubernetes consumable capacity](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/#consumable-capacity). | Implements Kubernetes DRA consumable capacity for full GPUs and MIG devices by publishing them as multi-allocatable and defining their capacity request policies; also set the [`consumableShares`](helm-values.md#feature-gates) Helm value to select an accounting mode; see [Consumable capacity](../guides/gpu-allocation/consumable-capacity.md). |\n\n## Constraints\n\nThe following feature gate combinations are mutually exclusive and cannot be enabled together.\n\n| Combination | Reason |\n| --- | --- |\n| `DynamicMIG` + `PassthroughSupport` | Mutually exclusive |\n| `DynamicMIG` + `MPSSupport` | Mutually exclusive |\n| `PassthroughSupport` + `NVMLDeviceHealthCheck` | Mutually exclusive |\n\nThe feature gates below have the following dependencies:\n\n| Feature gate | Requires |\n| --- | --- |\n| `ComputeDomainCliques` | `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` |\n| `DeviceMetadata` | `PassthroughSupport` |\n\n--- CONTROLLER HITS ---\npkg/featuregates/featuregates_test.go:449:\t\t\tfgMap: \"sigs.k8s.io/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/pkg/metrics\"\n\t\"sigs.k8s.io/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/pkg/workqueue\"\n)\n\n// computeDomainPublishedDevices returns the devices the kubelet plugin should\n// advertise in its node ResourceSlice. IMEX channel devices other than\n// channel 0 are never advertised here (they are advertised as a network\n// resource from the control plane instead). ComputeDomain daemon devices are\n// additionally omitted when hostManaged is true, since daemon claims are\n// never valid in that mode (see applyComputeDomainDaemonConfig).\nfunc computeDomainPublishedDevices(allocatable AllocatableDevices, hostManaged bool) []resourceapi.Device {\n\tvar devices []resourceapi.Device\n\tfor _, device := range allocatable {\n\t\tif device.Type() == ComputeDomainChannelType && device.Channel.ID != 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif hostManaged && device.Type() == ComputeDomainDaemonType {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tdevices = append(devices, device.GetDevice())\n\t}\n\treturn devices\n}\n\nconst (\n\t// ErrorRetryMaxTimeout limits the amount of time spent in the request\n\t// handlers UnprepareResourceClaims() and PrepareResourceClaims(), so that\n\t// we send a response to the kubelet in a predictable amount of time. Within\n\t// that deadline, retryable errors are retried (with backoff) via the\n\t// workqueue abstraction.\n\tErrorRetryMaxTimeout = 45 * time.Second\n\t// DriverPrepUprepFlockPath is the path to a lock file used to make sure\n\t// that calls to nodePrepareResource() / nodeUnprepareResource() never\n\t// interleave, node-globally.\n\tDriverPrepUprepFlockFileName = \"pu.lock\"\n)\n\n// permanentError defines an error indicating that it is permanent.\n// By default, every error will be retried up to ErrorRetryMaxTimeout.\n// Errors marked as permanent will not be retried.\ntype permanentError struct{ error }\n\nfunc isPermanentError(err error) bool {\n\treturn errors.As(err, &permanentError{})\n}\n\ntype driver struct {\n\tclient       coreclientset.Interface\n\tpluginhelper *kubeletplugin.Helper\n\tstate        *DeviceState\n\tpulock       *flock.Flock\n\thealthcheck  *healthcheck\n}\n\nfunc NewDriver(ctx context.Context, config *Config) (*driver, error) {\n\tstate, err := NewDeviceState(ctx, config)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tpuLockPath := filepath.Join(config.DriverPluginPath(), DriverPrepUprepFlockFileName)\n\n\tdriver := &driver{\n\t\tclient: config.clientsets.Core,\n\t\tstate:  state,\n\t\tpulock: flock.NewFlock(puLockPath),\n\t}\n\n\thelper, err := kubeletplugin.Start(\n\t\tctx,\n\t\tdriver,\n\t\tkubeletplugin.KubeClient(driver.client),\n\t\tkubeletplugin.NodeName(config.flags.nodeName),\n\t\tkubeletplugin.DriverName(DriverName),\n\t\t// By default, the DRA library serializes (un)prepare calls. That is, at\n\t\t// most one such call is exposed to the driver at any given time.\n\t\t// Disable that behavior: this driver has codependent prepare() actions\n\t\t// (where for the first prepare() to eventually complete, a second\n\t\t// prepare() must be incoming). Concurrency management for incoming\n\t\t// requests is done with this driver's work queue abstraction.\n\t\tkubeletplugin.Serialize(false),\n\t\tkubeletplugin.RegistrarDirectoryPath(config.flags.kubeletRegistrarDirectoryPath),\n\t\tkubeletplugin.PluginDataDirectoryPath(config.DriverPluginPath()),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdriver.pluginhelper = helper\n\n\t// Enumerate the set of ComputeDomain devices to publish.\n\tvar resourceSlice resourceslice.Slice\n\tresourceSlice.Devices = computeDomainPublishedDevices(state.allocatable, config.imexConfig.EffectiveHostManaged())\n\n\tresources := resourceslice.DriverResources{\n\t\tPools: map[string]resourceslice.Pool{\n\t\t\tconfig.flags.nodeName: {Slices: []resourceslice.Slice{resourceSlice}},\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\n\tif err := state.computeDomainManager.Start(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"error starting ComputeDomain manager: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Pass checkpoint mutation functions to the cleanup manager.\n\tif err := state.checkpointCleanupManager.Start(ctx, driver.nodeUnprepareResource, driver.deleteExpiredPrepareAbortedClaimEntries); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"error starting CheckpointCleanupManager: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\thealthcheck, err error {\n\tif d == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tif err := d.state.computeDomainManager.Stop(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"error stopping ComputeDomainManager: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif err (map[types.UID]kubeletplugin.PrepareResult, error) {\n\tklog.V(6).Infof(\"PrepareResourceClaims called with %d claim(s)\", len(claims))\n\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, ErrorRetryMaxTimeout)\n\tworkQueue := workqueue.New(workqueue.DefaultPrepUnprepRateLimiter())\n\tresults := make(map[types.UID]kubeletplugin.PrepareResult)\n\n\tfor _, claim := range claims {\n\t\twg.Add(1)\n\t\tkey := fmt.Sprintf(\"%v\", claim.UID)\n\t\tworkQueue.EnqueueRawWithKey(claim, key, func(ctx context.Context, obj any) error {\n\t\t\tdone, res := func() {\n\t\twg.Wait()\n\t\tcancel()\n\t}()\n\n\tworkQueue.Run(ctx)\n\treturn results, nil\n}\n\nfunc (d *driver) UnprepareResourceClaims(ctx context.Context, claimRefs workqueue.New(workqueue.DefaultPrepUnprepRateLimiter())\n\tresults := make(map[types.UID]error)\n\n\tfor _, claim := range claimRefs {\n\t\twg.Add(1)\n\t\tkey := fmt.Sprintf(\"%v\", *DeviceState) Prepare(ctx context.Context, claim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim) ([]kubeletplugin.Device, error) {\n\ts.Lock()\n\tdefer s.Unlock()\n\n\tclaimUID := string(claim.UID)\n\n\tcheckpoint, err preparedClaim.CheckpointState == ClaimCheckpointStatePrepareCompleted {\n\t\t// Associated device(s) has/ave been prepared by us. Prepare() must be\n\t\t// idempotent, as it may be invoked more than once per claim (and actual\n\t\t// device preparation must happen at most once).\n\t\tklog.V(4).Infof(\"Skip prepare: claim already in PrepareCompleted state: %s\", ResourceClaimToString(claim))\n\t\treturn preparedClaim.PreparedDevices.GetDevices(), nil\n\t}\n\tif exists && preparedClaim.CheckpointState == ClaimCheckpointStatePrepareAborted && claimMatchesPreparedClaim(preparedClaim, claim) {\n\t\treturn nil, permanentError{fmt.Errorf(\"stale prepare for claim %s: claim prepare was already aborted\", ResourceClaimToString(claim))}\n\t}\n\n\t// In certain scenarios, the same device can be prepared/allocated more than once for different claims\n\t// due to races between data processing in different goroutines in the scheduler, or when pods are\n\t// force-deleted while the kubelet still considers the devices allocated.\n\t// To prevent this, we check whether any device requested in the incoming claim has already been prepared\n\t// and fail the request if so (unless the prior preparation was performed with admin access).\n\t// More details: https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/pull/136269\n\tif err := s.validateNoOverlappingPreparedDevices(checkpoint, claim); That's deliberate, to annotate\n\t// potentially partially prepared claims. There is an asynchronous\n\t// checkpoint cleanup procedure that identifies when such entry goes stale.\n\terr = s.updateCheckpoint(func(checkpoint *Checkpoint) preparedDevices,\n\t\t\tName:            claim.Name,\n\t\t\tNamespace:       claim.Namespace,\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unable to update checkpoint: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tklog.V(6).Infof(\"checkpoint updated for claim %v\", claimUID)\n\n\treturn preparedDevices.GetDevices(), nil\n\n--- FILE cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/cdi.go ---\n/*\nCopyright The possible there are no GPUs available in NVML.\n\t// (Eg: All gpus prepared in passthrough-mode)\n\t// We use the INIT_FLAG_NO_GPUS flag to avoid failing if there are no GPUs.\n\tif r := cdi.nvml.InitWithFlags(nvml.INIT_FLAG_NO_GPUS); r != nvml.SUCCESS {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to initialize NVML: %v\", r)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() {\n\t\tif r := cdi.nvml.Shutdown(); r != nvml.SUCCESS {\n\t\t\tklog.Warningf(\"failed to shutdown NVML: %v\", r)\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t// Generate the set of common edits.\n\tcommonEdits, err := cdi.nvcdiDevice.GetCommonEdits()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get common CDI spec edits: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Make sure that NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES is set to void to avoid the\n\t// nvidia-container-runtime honoring it in addition to the underlying\n\t// runtime honoring CDI.\n\tcommonEdits.Env = append(\n\t\tcommonEdits.Env,\n\t\t\"NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=void\")\n\n\t// Generate device specs for all devices.\n\tdeviceSpecs, err := cdi.nvcdiDevice.GetDeviceSpecsByID(\"all\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"unable to get all GPU device specs: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Generate base spec from commonEdits and deviceEdits.\n\tspec, err := {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create CDI spec: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Transform the spec to make it aware that it is running inside a container.\n\terr = transformroot.New(\n\t\ttransformroot.WithRoot(cdi.driverRoot),\n\t\ttransformroot.WithTargetRoot(cdi.targetDriverRoot),\n\t\ttransformroot.WithRelativeTo(\"host\"),\n\t).Transform(spec.Raw())\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to transform driver root in CDI spec: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Update the spec to include only the minimum version necessary.\n\tminVersion, err := cdispec.MinimumRequiredVersion(spec.Raw())\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get minimum required CDI spec version: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tspec.Raw().Version = minVersion\n\n\t// Write the spec out to disk.\n\tspecName := cdiapi.GenerateTransientSpecName(cdiVendor, cdiDeviceClass, cdiBaseSpecIdentifier)\n\treturn cdi.cache.WriteSpec(spec.Raw(), specName)\n}\n\nfunc (cdi *CDIHandler) {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_20","type":"agent_message","text":"Using `dra-compute-domain-imex-workflows`, here’s the driver-managed path end to end.\n\n**Big Picture**\n- In `driverManaged` mode, creating a `ComputeDomain` causes the DRA driver to create a per-domain `nvidia-imex` DaemonSet, track daemon readiness through `ComputeDomainClique` objects, and create a workload-facing `ResourceClaimTemplate` for IMEX channel claims. See `site/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:21`, `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:126`, `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:165`.\n- The architecture doc summarizes the sequence as: `ComputeDomain` → per-domain DaemonSet → daemon publishes clique state → workload claims from `compute-domain-default-channel.nvidia.com` → kubelet plugin verifies readiness and injects channel devices. See `site/content/docs/concepts/architecture.md:70`.\n\n**1. User creates the `ComputeDomain`**\n- The user creates a `resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1` `ComputeDomain` with `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate.name`, and optionally `spec.channel.allocationMode`. See `site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md:17`, `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:146`.\n- The API contract says this CR “provisions an ephemeral multi-node NVLink fabric” and triggers controller creation of daemon fleet plus channel claim template. See `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:126`.\n\n**2. Controller reconciles the new CR**\n- The main reconciliation lives in `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go`. In driver-managed mode, `NewComputeDomainManager` wires up both the DaemonSet manager and the workload `ResourceClaimTemplate` manager; those are skipped only for host-managed IMEX. See `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:64`, `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:91`.\n- On reconcile, the controller:\n  - adds a finalizer,\n  - creates the per-`ComputeDomain` DaemonSet,\n  - creates the workload `ResourceClaimTemplate`,\n  - updates global status.  \n  See `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:398`, `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:409`, `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:413`, `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:418`.\n- The workload-facing DeviceClass name is the default channel class `compute-domain-default-channel.nvidia.com`. See `cmd/compute-domain-controller/computedomain.go:54`.\n\n**3. The controller-created `ResourceClaimTemplate` represents the channel request**\n- Conceptually, this template is what workload pods later reference to get a channel claim. The docs call this out directly: the controller generates `ResourceClaimTemplate` objects that workload pods reference to claim IMEX channels. See `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:126`.\n- The channel config carries the `ComputeDomain` identity and allocation mode through opaque config using `ComputeDomainChannelConfig`. See `api/nvidia.com/resource/v1beta1/computedomainconfig.go:51`.\n- `allocationMode` controls whether a claim gets one channel (`Single`) or the domain’s full set (`All`). See `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:147`, `site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md:97`.\n\n**4. Per-node daemon pods come up and supervise `nvidia-imex`**\n- Each pod in the per-domain DaemonSet runs `cmd/compute-domain-daemon`. That binary defines its IMEX config directory as `/imexd`, with files like `/imexd/imexd.cfg` and `/imexd/nodes.cfg`. See `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/main.go:45`.\n- The docs describe this step as “each daemon pod in that DaemonSet runs `nvidia-imex`, which manages the NVLink fabric connection on its node.” See `site/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:24`.\n- So `/imexd` first exists on the daemon side as the daemon’s config/runtime directory, not yet in the workload container. See `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/main.go:45`.\n\n**5. Daemons publish clique membership and readiness**\n- In driver-managed mode, each daemon publishes IP/clique/readiness via `ComputeDomainClique`. The docs say these are driver-managed resources created automatically by the IMEX daemon on each node, named `<computeDomainUID>.<cliqueID>`, and used to track daemon readiness. See `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:167`, `site/content/docs/reference/api.md:169`.\n- The daemon code has a dedicated `ComputeDomainCliqueManager` that watches/creates/updates the clique object for this domain/clique combination. See `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/cdclique.go:30`.\n- Feature-gate-wise, `ComputeDomainCliques` depends on `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`, and both are part of the normal driver-managed path. See `site/content/docs/reference/feature-gates.md:37`, `site/content/docs/reference/feature-gates.md:41`.\n\n**6. Workload pod references the channel template**\n- A workload pod uses the controller-created template by name; Kubernetes then creates/allocates a `ResourceClaim` against the `compute-domain-default-channel.nvidia.com` DeviceClass. This is the workload boundary the docs emphasize. See `site/content/docs/guides/compute-domain-workloads.md:38`, `site/content/docs/concepts/architecture.md:70`.\n- At this point the control plane has selected the channel device(s), but the pod still does not see them until kubelet prepare succeeds.\n\n**7. Kubelet plugin prepares the claim on the target node**\n- The local prepare path is in `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/device_state.go`. `Prepare()` records checkpoint state, calls `prepareDevices()`, then writes a CDI claim spec with `CreateClaimSpecFile()`. See `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/device_state.go:184`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/device_state.go:233`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/device_state.go:238`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/cdi.go:208`.\n- For channel claims, the kubelet plugin validates that:\n  - the claim belongs to the right `ComputeDomain`,\n  - the claim namespace matches the `ComputeDomain` namespace,\n  - the current node is ready for that `ComputeDomain`.  \n  The readiness check uses `ComputeDomainClique` first when enabled, then falls back to `ComputeDomain.status.nodes`. See `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:323`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:341`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:358`.\n\n**8. What gets injected into the workload**\n- The docs state the plugin injects one or more selected IMEX channel devices as `/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channelN`. See `site/content/docs/concepts/architecture.md:70`, `site/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:28`.\n- The CDI layer is how this becomes container-visible: `CreateClaimSpecFile()` writes claim-specific CDI edits for the prepared devices, and kubelet/runtime consume that spec for container injection. See `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/cdi.go:208`.\n- The kubelet-plugin code also prepares per-domain daemon config under its own plugin state and includes a template path for the daemon config; comments in the code explicitly tie this to the daemon’s `/imexd` config directory. See `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:57`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:235`.\n- So when you say the workload “sees `/imexd` and channel devices,” the practical meaning is:\n  - the workload gets the IMEX channel character device nodes via CDI, and\n  - the daemon-related config/runtime path needed for the ComputeDomain daemon integration is surfaced through the plugin-managed container edits/pathing that corresponds to the daemon’s `/imexd` layout. The daemon itself defines `/imexd` as its config root. See `cmd/compute-domain-daemon/main.go:45`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/cdi.go:208`.\n\n**What actually gates success**\n- The important operational boundary is not just `ComputeDomain.status`. The skill is explicit about this, and the code matches it: the real gate for a workload is successful local prepare plus local IMEX readiness evidence. See `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/computedomain.go:323`, `cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin/device_state.go:184`.\n- In short: a `Ready` `ComputeDomain` is informative, but the workload only sees `/imexd`-related setup and IMEX channel devices after the kubelet plugin can prove the node’s daemon is ready for that domain and write the CDI claim spec.\n\n**Compact sequence**\n- Create `ComputeDomain`.\n- Controller adds finalizer, creates per-domain IMEX DaemonSet, creates workload `ResourceClaimTemplate`.\n- Daemon pods start `nvidia-imex` and use `/imexd` as their config/runtime directory.\n- Daemons publish membership/readiness into `ComputeDomainClique`.\n- Workload references the channel template and gets a channel `ResourceClaim`.\n- Kubelet plugin `Prepare()` verifies claim/domain/node readiness.\n- Plugin writes CDI claim spec.\n- Container runtime injects `/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channelN` and the daemon-related `/imexd` pathing/config exposure needed for the ComputeDomain flow.\n\nIf you want, I can turn this into a one-page sequence diagram or walk through the exact objects created for a sample `ComputeDomain` manifest."}}
